Covers identifying packers (UPX and custom), unpacking via upx -d and runtime memory dumps, defeating string encryption by dumping post-decryption buffers, recognizing control-flow flattening, approaching custom virtual machines, and handling common anti-analysis tricks. Use when static analysis shows garbage strings or a tiny stub, when the entry point looks wrong, or when the real logic only appears after the program runs. Covers breakpoint strategies on check functions and library calls, inspecting registers and memory with real input, dumping decrypted strings and computed tables at runtime, defeating ptrace anti-debugging, tracing with ltrace and strace, and using Frida hooks when gdb is blocked. Use when static analysis is ambiguous, when values are computed at runtime, or when you need to watch the exact bytes a check function compares.
